You improve a harness that runs a frozen video model.

The harness answers one kind of request: "As an emotion recognition expert, identify
the most prominent emotion displayed in the video. Candidate labels: ... ." The same
wording occurs with "sentiment" in place of "emotion". Answers must use labels copied
verbatim from the candidate list of the request the harness is given.

Your objective, in priority order:
1. raise the share of construction items whose emitted label matches the gold label;
2. keep every item the current harness already answers correctly;
3. keep the mean model calls per item and the p95 latency within budget.

Rules:
- Reply with one JSON object and nothing else, of exactly this shape:
  {"summary": "...", "candidates": [{"name": "...", "targeted_cluster": "...",
  "rationale": "...", "files": [{"path": "...", "content": "..."}]}]}
- Every "content" is the complete new text of that file. Diffs and partial files are
  rejected. A file you do not list stays as it is.
- Each candidate targets exactly one cluster and repeats that cluster's key verbatim
  in "targeted_cluster".
- Propose at most 4 candidates; each is evaluated on its own.
- You may replace only the paths listed under "Editable paths" below. A candidate that
  touches any other path is rejected before it is evaluated.
- The five-step answer format and the code that emits the final answer are fixed.

Stage: 2. Round: 3.
Current construction-set accuracy: 0.4250.

Editable paths:
- audio_pipeline.yaml
- prompts/extract_visual.md
- prompts/generate_hypotheses.md
- prompts/synthesize.md
- prompts/verify.md
- routing.yaml
- sampling.yaml

## Current harness files

### audio_pipeline.yaml
```
# Audio channels the harness consumes (ADR-0004). Both are produced by the
# preprocessing cache; the harness only decides how much of them to show.
# channels: 'cues' is the observable-cue channel, 'opinion' the audio-model voter.
# transcript_max_chars truncates a long transcript before it enters the record.
channels:
  - cues
  - opinion
transcript_max_chars: 2000

```

### prompts/extract_visual.md
```
<!-- Rendered with str.format_map. Placeholders: {noun}. -->
<!-- Optional placeholders this stage also accepts: {prompt}, {quantifier}, {label_set}. -->
<!-- A literal brace must be written twice: {{ becomes { and }} becomes }. -->

You are given a short video clip. Describe only what can be seen in it.

Return a single JSON object and nothing else, with these keys:

- `face`: a list of short strings, each one visible cue from the face or gaze.
- `body`: a list of short strings, each one visible cue from posture, hands, or movement.
- `scene`: a list of short strings, each one fact about the setting, the other people, or what is happening.
- `temporal_change`: one short string describing how the visible cues change between the start and the end of the clip, or `null` when they do not change.

Rules:

- Report observations, not conclusions. Write what a cue looks like, not what it means.
- Do not state the {noun} displayed in the video, and do not answer any question about the clip.
- Write each string as a short phrase of at most fifteen words. Leave a list empty rather than guessing.
- Never describe something you cannot see, and never describe sound.
- Output JSON only: no prose before or after it, no code fence, no comments.

The strings you return become numbered evidence items in the order `face`, `body`, `scene`, `temporal_change`, so that later steps can cite them as v1, v2, v3 and so on. Do not number them yourself.

```

### prompts/generate_hypotheses.md
```
<!-- Rendered with str.format_map. Placeholders: {noun}, {label_set}, {evidence_json}. -->
<!-- Optional placeholders this stage also accepts: {prompt}, {quantifier}, {audio_text}. -->
<!-- A literal brace must be written twice: {{ becomes { and }} becomes }. -->

Below is the evidence record collected from one video clip.

EVIDENCE RECORD

{evidence_json}

How to read the record:

- `items` lists every citable observation with its id and its modality. An id starting with `v` came from what was seen, one starting with `a` from what was heard, `o` from a separate audio model, and `t` from the wording of the question.
- An `audio_opinion` item is an opinion, not an observation. Treat it as one voter that can be wrong, and never cite it as something that was seen or heard.
- An empty transcript means no intelligible speech was recorded. Do not invent words that are not there.

The question asks for the {noun} displayed in the video.
The answer must be copied from this list, character for character: {label_set}

Return a single JSON object and nothing else, with these keys:

- `coarse`: one short phrase for the overall state you read from the record, in your own words.
- `hypotheses`: a list of objects, each with
  - `label`: one entry from the list above, copied exactly as written there,
  - `support`: a list of item ids from the record that speak for that entry,
  - `contradiction`: a list of item ids from the record that speak against it,
  - `confidence`: a number between 0 and 1.

Rules:

- Use only ids that appear in `items`. Never invent an id and never cite an id twice in the same list.
- Order the hypotheses from most to least likely. Two to four of them is usual.
- A label with no supporting id is allowed only when nothing in the record supports anything.
- Output JSON only: no prose before or after it, no code fence, no comments.

```

### prompts/synthesize.md
```
<!-- Rendered with str.format_map. Placeholders: {noun}, {label_set}, {evidence_json}, {hypotheses_json}, {step_titles}. -->
<!-- Optional placeholders this stage also accepts: {prompt}, {quantifier}, {audio_text}. -->
<!-- A literal brace must be written twice: {{ becomes { and }} becomes }. -->

Write the reasoning for one video clip using only the record below.

EVIDENCE RECORD

{evidence_json}

RANKED READINGS

{hypotheses_json}

The question asks for the {noun} displayed in the video.
The entries under consideration are: {label_set}

Write one short paragraph for each of these headings, in this order:

{step_titles}

Rules:

- Every sentence must cite at least one evidence id in square brackets, written as `[v2]` or `[a1, o1]`. A sentence with no citation is deleted before anyone reads the answer.
- Cite only ids that appear in `items`. An invented id is deleted the same way.
- Keep each paragraph to two or three sentences and do not repeat a heading inside its paragraph.
- The last paragraph must name the strongest alternative reading and say what speaks against it.
- Do not write a closing sentence and do not state the final answer; that sentence is written for you.

Return a single JSON object and nothing else, with these keys:

- `steps`: a list of exactly four strings, one paragraph per heading, in the order above.
- `justification`: one sentence, with citations, saying why the top-ranked reading beats the strongest alternative.

Output JSON only: no prose before or after it, no code fence, no comments.

```

### prompts/verify.md
```
<!-- Rendered with str.format_map. Placeholders: {noun}, {label_set}, {audio_text}, {evidence_json}, {hypotheses_json}. -->
<!-- Optional placeholders this stage also accepts: {prompt}, {quantifier}. -->
<!-- A literal brace must be written twice: {{ becomes { and }} becomes }. -->

Re-examine an earlier reading of one video clip. Start from what was heard, then check it against what was seen.

WHAT WAS HEARD

{audio_text}

FULL EVIDENCE RECORD

{evidence_json}

EARLIER RANKING

{hypotheses_json}

The question asks for the {noun} displayed in the video.
The answer must be copied from this list, character for character: {label_set}

Calibration for the happy | neutral | agree error pattern:

- Apply this rule when the question asks for emotion and the candidate list contains the exact labels `happy` and `neutral`.
- An explicit visible smile, smiling mouth, smiling eyes, or a sustained pleasant facial expression is positive evidence for `happy`, even when the smile is slight.
- Do not treat no laughter, flat speech, moderate volume, or an audio-model suggestion of `neutral` as evidence against a visible smile. The audio opinion is only one fallible voter.
- Prefer `neutral` only when the visible expression is explicitly neutral or when there is no meaningful positive facial or behavioral cue. If `happy` is missing from the earlier ranking, add it from the candidate list when the record supports it.
- Copy whichever exact candidate-list spelling is selected; never output the calibration wording itself.

Work in this order:

1. Decide what the heard cues on their own point to, ignoring the earlier ranking.
2. Decide what the seen cues on their own point to.
3. Compare the two readings, then re-order and re-score the ranking.

Return a single JSON object and nothing else, with these keys:

- `modalities_agree`: `true` when the heard cues and the seen cues point to the same entry, `false` when they point to different entries.
- `hypotheses`: the same kind of list as the earlier ranking, with the keys `label`, `support`, `contradiction`, and `confidence`, re-ordered and re-scored after this second reading.

Rules:

- Keep every label exactly as it is written in the list above.
- Use only ids that appear in the record; drop any id you cannot find there.
- When the two readings disagree, say so through `modalities_agree` and lower the confidences; do not hide the conflict by dropping a hypothesis.
- An opinion item is one voter, not an observation.
- Output JSON only: no prose before or after it, no code fence, no comments.

```

### routing.yaml
```
# Which modules run, in which order, and which deterministic checks they use.
# stages must be a subset of the pipeline's known stage names, in order.
# verification.agreement_check names the module under verification/ that exports
# modalities_agree; verification.ranker names the one that exports rank.
# audio.use_opinion decides whether the audio-model opinion enters the evidence
# record as a citable item (ADR-0004 §2); it is never treated as an observation.
stages:
  - extract_visual
  - generate_hypotheses
  - verify
  - synthesize
verification:
  agreement_check: cross_modal
  ranker: alternative_hypotheses
audio:
  use_opinion: true

```

### sampling.yaml
```
# Frame and clip selection policy (ADR-0011 §3).
# face_crops.enabled adds up to max_crops face-crop images to the visual stage.
# extra_images.enabled additionally attaches the cached 2 fps frames.
face_crops:
  enabled: false
  max_crops: 4
extra_images:
  enabled: false

```

### verification/alternative_hypotheses.py
```
"""Alternative-hypothesis ranking (ADR-0005: no calibration, no label priors).

Stage 1 rule: order by stated confidence, then by how many supporting items exist
in the record, then by the order the hypotheses arrived in.

This file is loaded through the static allowlist in
``emoharness.harness.allowlist``: no imports beyond ``__future__`` and ``typing``,
no attribute access, no loops other than ``for``, and calls only to whitelisted
builtins or to functions defined here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any


def rank(record: dict[str, Any], hypotheses: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Hypotheses ordered from strongest to weakest."""
    rows = []
    index = 0
    for hypothesis in hypothesis_list(hypotheses):
        confidence = confidence_of(hypothesis)
        support = support_count(record, hypothesis)
        rows = rows + [(-confidence, -support, index, hypothesis)]
        index = index + 1
    ordered = sorted(rows)
    return [row[3] for row in ordered]


def confidence_of(hypothesis: dict[str, Any]) -> float:
    """The stated confidence, or 0.0 when there is none."""
    if 'confidence' in hypothesis:
        return float(hypothesis['confidence'])
    return 0.0


def support_count(record: dict[str, Any], hypothesis: dict[str, Any]) -> int:
    """How many supporting ids of a hypothesis exist in the record."""
    known = known_ids(record)
    count = 0
    for item_id in id_list(hypothesis, 'support'):
        if str(item_id) in known:
            count = count + 1
    return count


def known_ids(record: dict[str, Any]) -> set[str]:
    """Every citable id in the record."""
    return {str(item['id']) for item in item_list(record)}


def item_list(record: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Any]:
    """The record's citable items, or an empty list."""
    if 'items' in record:
        return list(record['items'])
    return []


def hypothesis_list(hypotheses: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Any]:
    """The hypothesis objects, or an empty list."""
    if 'hypotheses' in hypotheses:
        return list(hypotheses['hypotheses'])
    return []


def id_list(hypothesis: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> list[Any]:
    """One id list of a hypothesis, or an empty list."""
    if key in hypothesis:
        return list(hypothesis[key])
    return []

```

### verification/cross_modal.py
```
"""Cross-modal agreement flag (ADR-0005: one deterministic check, no resolution rule).

Stage 1 rule: the label best supported by seen items must equal the label best
supported by heard items. When one side supports nothing, there is nothing to
disagree with and the flag stays true.

This file is loaded through the static allowlist in
``emoharness.harness.allowlist``: no imports beyond ``__future__`` and ``typing``,
no attribute access, no loops other than ``for``, and calls only to whitelisted
builtins or to functions defined here.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

SEEN_MODALITIES = ('visual',)
HEARD_MODALITIES = ('audio', 'audio_opinion')


def modalities_agree(record: dict[str, Any], hypotheses: dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
    """True when the seen cues and the heard cues favour the same label."""
    seen = top_label(record, hypotheses, SEEN_MODALITIES)
    heard = top_label(record, hypotheses, HEARD_MODALITIES)
    if seen == '' or heard == '':
        return True
    return seen == heard


def top_label(
    record: dict[str, Any],
    hypotheses: dict[str, Any],
    modalities: tuple[str, ...],
) -> str:
    """The label with the most supporting items in ``modalities``; '' when none."""
    rows = []
    index = 0
    for hypothesis in hypothesis_list(hypotheses):
        count = support_count(record, hypothesis, modalities)
        rows = rows + [(count, -index, str(hypothesis['label']))]
        index = index + 1
    ordered = sorted(rows, reverse=True)
    if len(ordered) == 0:
        return ''
    if ordered[0][0] == 0:
        return ''
    return str(ordered[0][2])


def support_count(
    record: dict[str, Any],
    hypothesis: dict[str, Any],
    modalities: tuple[str, ...],
) -> int:
    """How many of a hypothesis' supporting ids exist and sit in ``modalities``."""
    count = 0
    for item_id in id_list(hypothesis, 'support'):
        if modality_of(record, str(item_id)) in modalities:
            count = count + 1
    return count


def modality_of(record: dict[str, Any], item_id: str) -> str:
    """The modality of one evidence item, or '' when the id is unknown."""
    for item in item_list(record):
        if str(item['id']) == item_id:
            return str(item['modality'])
    return ''


def item_list(record: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Any]:
    """The record's citable items, or an empty list."""
    if 'items' in record:
        return list(record['items'])
    return []


def hypothesis_list(hypotheses: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Any]:
    """The hypothesis objects, or an empty list."""
    if 'hypotheses' in hypotheses:
        return list(hypotheses['hypotheses'])
    return []


def id_list(hypothesis: dict[str, Any], key: str) -> list[Any]:
    """One id list of a hypothesis, or an empty list."""
    if key in hypothesis:
        return list(hypothesis[key])
    return []

```
